`README.md`:

```md
# Scorpio crosshair overlay

Scorpio is a free crosshair overlay for PC games on Windows and Linux. It draws a CS2-based crosshair in its own window over your game, shows it only while a game is in front, and never opens, reads, hooks or injects into the game or its anti-cheat.

<p align="center">
  <picture>
    <source media="(prefers-color-scheme: dark)" srcset="assets/brand/scorpio-lockup.svg">
    <img src="assets/brand/scorpio-lockup-dark.svg" alt="Scorpio logo: two interlaced bands around a red point of aim, above the name SCORPIO" width="300">
  </picture>
</p>

<p align="center">
  <b><a href="https://github.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/releases/latest">Download Scorpio</a></b><br>
  Windows installer, Linux AppImage and <code>.deb</code>. Free for non-commercial use.
</p>

<p align="center">
  <a href="https://scorpio.lxxvll.com/">Website</a> ·
  <a href="#is-scorpio-safe-with-anti-cheats">Anti-cheat safety</a> ·
  <a href="#faq">FAQ</a>
</p>

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/images/scorpio-saved-and-pro-crosshairs.webp" alt="Scorpio settings window: a green crosshair in the preview at 4x, its share code, four saved crosshairs and pro player crosshairs" width="820">
</p>

| | |
| --- | --- |
| What it is | A crosshair overlay: a transparent, click-through window on top of the game |
| Systems | Windows 10 and 11. Linux with X11 or XWayland |
| Crosshair | CS2-based, five static styles; CS2 crosshair codes work |
| When it shows | Only while a game is the window in front |
| Memory and CPU | 6.9 MB of private memory and 0 ms of CPU time over 10 s while it waits (measured on Windows 11) |
| Price | Free |
| Licence | PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0. Source-available, free for non-commercial use |
| Written in | Rust, with Tauri 2 |
| Version | 0.1.1 |

Scorpio is new. [What is tested and what is not](#what-is-tested-and-what-is-not) lists what has been proven so far.

## Features

### The crosshair shows only over games

Scorpio waits in the tray and shows the crosshair when the window in front is a game. The crosshair disappears at once when you switch to another window. A window counts as a game in three ways:

- **You added it.** Pick the game from the list of open windows, or press `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+G` while the game is in front. Scorpio stores four checks for the game: program name, program path, window title and window class. Two matching checks are enough, so a changed window title or a moved folder does not break the match.
- **It is fullscreen.** Any fullscreen or borderless window counts, except the desktop.
- **A launcher installed it.** Scorpio reads the install folders that the launchers record. On Windows these are Steam, Epic Games, GOG, Ubisoft Connect, EA, Battle.net, Riot and Xbox. On Linux these are Steam, Heroic and Lutris.

The second and the third way each have a switch. Both are on by default.

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/images/scorpio-game-detection.webp" alt="Scorpio Games tab: two added games, one with its own crosshair, and switches for fullscreen windows and games from launchers" width="820">
</p>

### CS2-based crosshairs

- **Five static styles.** Static Cross, Static Circle, Dot Only, Static Square and Static Quad.
- **The settings you know.** Length, thickness, gap, centre dot, T shape, colour, opacity and outline.
- **CS2 crosshair codes work in Scorpio.** Paste a code to import it; Scorpio reads codes that start with `CSGO-` or `CS`. Copy gives a code you can paste back.
- **Finer in two places.** Thickness moves in half steps. The outline can be 1 to 6 pixels wide. A share code carries neither, so thickness is rounded in a copied code and the outline width stays in Scorpio.
- **Static only.** A code with a dynamic style is shown as the static style it rests as. An overlay cannot know when you move or shoot.
- **13 pro crosshairs.** ropz, b1t, jL, Twistzz, XANTARES, Spinx, iM, w0nderful, karrigan, apEX, sh1ro, flameZ and mezii. The codes were seen in their matches and confirmed by a second source. They do not come from the players, the teams or Valve.
- **Saved crosshairs.** Save up to 100, rename them, and step through them with a shortcut.
- **A crosshair per game.** An added game can have its own crosshair. You edit it in the same editor.
- **Exact centring.** Scorpio places the crosshair on the centre of the monitor that the game is on, between the two centre pixels where a game draws its own crosshair. At 125 % or 150 % display scaling it picks the window size whose middle lands closest. You can also unlock the crosshair, drag it anywhere, and lock it again.

There are no image crosshairs, on purpose.

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/images/scorpio-crosshair-editor.webp" alt="Scorpio Crosshair tab: preview with a share code field, and sliders for style, length, thickness in half steps, and gap" width="820">
</p>

### Controls

**Hide while aiming** is for scoped weapons. Scorpio hides the crosshair while you hold the aim button, or switches it on each press. You choose the button: right, middle, back, forward or left. Scorpio reads the button through raw input, the way a game does, and sends nothing to the game. The setting is off by default.

Global shortcuts work while the game is in front:

| Action | Default shortcut |
| --- | --- |
| Unlock or lock the position | `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+X` |
| Centre exactly | `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+C` |
| Hide or show | `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+H` |
| Next saved crosshair | `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+.` |
| Previous saved crosshair | `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+,` |
| Add the game in front | `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+G` |

You can change every shortcut. A shortcut needs Ctrl, Alt or Super unless it is an F-key, so it cannot take a key that the game uses.

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/images/scorpio-hide-while-aiming-and-shortcuts.webp" alt="Scorpio Controls tab: the hide while aiming switch, the aim button, hold or toggle mode, and the six global shortcuts" width="820">
</p>

### Stream, updates and weight

- **OBS.** An opt-in Browser source puts the crosshair into your stream, also when OBS records with Game Capture. See [Show the crosshair in OBS](#show-the-crosshair-in-obs).
- **Updates between games.** Scorpio checks for a new version 30 seconds after it starts and then every 6 hours. It installs an update only after no game has been in front for 3 minutes, so a restart never lands in a match. Update packages are signed. You can switch automatic updates off and use the **Check for updates** button.
- **Light.** While it waits in the tray, Scorpio uses 6.9 MB of private memory and 0 ms of CPU time over 10 seconds. Every input is an event, and nothing polls. The settings window exists only while it is open. `scorpio.exe` is 3.7 MB. The figures are in [docs/budget.md](docs/budget.md), and a check fails when one of them grows by more than 25 %.

## Is Scorpio safe with anti-cheats?

Scorpio is built so that it has no contact with the game. It is a separate window on top of the game, and it works only with what the operating system tells every program about windows and input. No overlay can promise that you will never be banned. No anti-cheat vendor has approved Scorpio directly, but Scorpio crosses none of their lines: it never touches the game or the anti-cheat. What follows is what Scorpio does and does not do on your computer, so you can judge for yourself.

Scorpio never does any of the following:

- open a handle to another process (`OpenProcess` is banned in the code)
- read or write another process's memory
- inject a DLL or any other code
- install a hook of any kind, including low-level keyboard and mouse hooks
- send synthetic input (`SendInput`, `keybd_event`)
- load a kernel driver
- hook DirectX or Vulkan, or draw into the game's frames
- scan the modules of other processes

This table lists every operating system call that Scorpio makes to do its work.

| Need | Windows | Linux (X11) | Why it stays outside the game |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Which window is in front | `SetWinEventHook(EVENT_SYSTEM_FOREGROUND, WINEVENT_OUTOFCONTEXT)` | `PropertyNotify` on the root window's `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` | Out of context, so nothing is loaded into another process. An event, not a poll |
| The window's process id, title, class and size | `GetWindowThreadProcessId`, `GetWindowTextW`, `GetClassNameW`, `GetWindowRect`, `MonitorFromWindow` | `_NET_WM_PID`, `WM_CLASS`, `_NET_WM_NAME`, `_NET_WM_STATE` | Window manager data. No process is opened |
| The program's path | `NtQuerySystemInformation(SystemProcessIdInformation)` | `/proc/<pid>/exe`, or `cmdline` for Wine | No process handle is opened |
| Aim button and hide key | Raw Input with `RIDEV_INPUTSINK` on Scorpio's own hidden window | XInput2 raw events on the root window | The input API that games themselves read. No hooks |
| Shortcuts | `RegisterHotKey` | `XGrabKey` | The standard API for global shortcuts |
| The crosshair | A layered, topmost, click-through window | An override-redirect ARGB window with an empty input shape | A separate window. Nothing is drawn into the game |
| Colour pipette | One screen grab when you click **Pick** | Not available | Only on request |
| Crosshair in OBS, opt-in | An HTTP server on `127.0.0.1:47117` | The same | Talks only to OBS on the same computer. No thread or socket while it is off |
| Hide from screen capture, opt-in | `SetWindowDisplayAffinity(WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE)` on Scorpio's own window | Not available | See the exception below |

### The one exception: Hide from screen capture

**Hide from screen capture** is a switch on the System tab, for Windows only. When it is on, screenshots, recordings and streams show the game without the crosshair.

This switch is not risk-free, and it is off by default. Cheat overlays use the same Windows call to hide from the screenshots that an anti-cheat takes. An anti-cheat that sees a hidden window over the game could take a closer look at Scorpio. Leave the switch off unless you need it. To keep the crosshair in a stream, or to keep it out of one, use the [OBS source](#show-the-crosshair-in-obs) instead: it needs no hidden window.

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/images/scorpio-system-hide-from-capture.webp" alt="Scorpio System tab: the Hide from screen capture switch is off, with a warning that cheat overlays hide the same way" width="820">
</p>

### What this does not cover

A game's terms or a tournament's rules can forbid external crosshairs whatever the technique. Scorpio's design removes the technical contact with the game. It does not change those rules. Check the rules of the game that you play.

## Install

### Windows

1. Open the [latest release](https://github.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/releases/latest) and download the file that ends in `-setup.exe`.
2. Run the installer. It installs for your user account and does not ask for administrator rights.
3. Scorpio starts in the tray. Click the tray icon to open the settings window.

The installer is not signed with a Windows code-signing certificate, so Windows SmartScreen can show a warning the first time. The settings window uses Microsoft Edge WebView2. Windows 11 includes it, and the installer downloads it when it is missing.

### Linux

Scorpio needs X11 or XWayland. On a Wayland desktop it runs through XWayland, and it sees only games that are X11 windows. A game that runs as a native Wayland window is invisible to it. Wayland without XWayland is not supported.

**AppImage.** Download the `.AppImage` file from the [latest release](https://github.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/releases/latest), make it executable, and run it. The AppImage updates itself.

```sh
chmod +x Scorpio_*.AppImage
./Scorpio_*.AppImage
```

**Debian and Ubuntu.** Download the `.deb` file and install it. A `.deb` install does not update itself. Install a newer `.deb` to update.

```sh
sudo apt install ./Scorpio_*.deb
```

Two things differ from Windows. **Hide from screen capture** is disabled, because X11 has no way to keep a window out of captures. The **Pick** button of the colour picker is absent, because WebKitGTK has no pipette.

### First steps

1. Click the tray icon. The settings window opens on the **Crosshair** tab.
2. Paste a CS2 crosshair code into **Share code**, or set the crosshair with the sliders.
3. Open the **Games** tab and click **Add a game**. You can skip this step for a fullscreen game or a game from a launcher.
4. Switch to the game. The crosshair shows. The line in the title bar of the settings window says what the overlay does now, for example "Hidden until a game is in front".

## Show the crosshair in OBS

OBS Game Capture reads the game's own frames, so a crosshair in a separate window is never in the recording. Scorpio can serve the same crosshair to OBS as a Browser source. The source shows and hides together with the crosshair on your screen.

1. In Scorpio, open the **System** tab and switch on **Show the crosshair in OBS**.
2. In OBS, add a **Browser** source above your game capture.
3. Set the URL of the source to `http://127.0.0.1:47117/obs`.
4. Set the width and the height of the source to your canvas size. OBS shows the canvas size under **Settings**, **Video**, **Base (Canvas) Resolution**.
5. Keep the source filling the whole canvas, with no transform.

Scorpio scales the crosshair from the height of your game's screen to the height of the canvas, so it lands where it does on your screen. The server listens on `127.0.0.1` only, so nothing outside your computer can reach it. While the switch is off, Scorpio opens no socket and runs no server thread.

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/images/scorpio-obs-browser-source.webp" alt="Scorpio System tab: Show the crosshair in OBS is on, with three setup steps and the Browser source address http://127.0.0.1:47117/obs" width="820">
</p>

## FAQ

### Is a crosshair overlay bannable?

It depends on the game's rules, and no overlay can promise that you will never be banned. Anti-cheats look for programs that read or change a game. Scorpio does neither. It is a separate transparent window on top of the game, and it never opens the game's process, reads its memory, hooks it, injects code or sends input. A game's terms can still forbid external crosshairs, so check the rules of the game that you play. No anti-cheat vendor has approved Scorpio directly, but it crosses none of their lines. See [Is Scorpio safe with anti-cheats?](#is-scorpio-safe-with-anti-cheats)

### Does Scorpio work with Vanguard, Easy Anti-Cheat, BattlEye or VAC?

None of them has approved it directly. Scorpio crosses none of their lines, since it never touches the game's process or the anti-cheat's process; the full list of its system calls is in the [safety section](#is-scorpio-safe-with-anti-cheats). The exception is a game whose own rules forbid crosshair overlays.

### Do CS2 crosshair codes work in Scorpio?

Yes. Copy the code from your game's crosshair settings or from a site that lists pro crosshairs. In Scorpio, open the **Crosshair** tab and paste the code into **Share code**. Scorpio draws the same crosshair over any game it detects. A dynamic crosshair is shown in its static resting style.

### Can I take my Scorpio crosshair back into the game?

Yes. Click **Copy** next to the share code and import the code in the game. Thickness in half steps is rounded in the code, and an outline wider than 1 pixel stays in Scorpio, because share codes carry neither.

### Does Scorpio work on Linux?

Scorpio is built for Linux with X11 or XWayland, as an AppImage and a `.deb`. It is less proven there than on Windows: the X11 layer has run under a virtual X server, and the full app has not been tested on a Linux desktop or in a game yet. Native Wayland windows are not supported.

### Does the crosshair show on my stream or recording?

With OBS Display Capture it does, because Display Capture records the screen. With OBS Game Capture it does not, until you add Scorpio's Browser source. See [Show the crosshair in OBS](#show-the-crosshair-in-obs). To keep the crosshair out of every capture on Windows, there is **Hide from screen capture**, with the risk described in the safety section.

### Why does the crosshair not show over my game?

Check these in order:

1. The title bar of the settings window says "Hidden by shortcut". Press the hide shortcut again.
2. Scorpio does not see the window as a game. Add the game on the **Games** tab.
3. The game runs in exclusive fullscreen. Windows may not draw other windows over an exclusive fullscreen game. Switch the game to borderless or windowed fullscreen.
4. On Linux, the game runs as a native Wayland window. Scorpio sees only X11 windows.

### Does Scorpio lower my frame rate or add input lag?

Scorpio does not touch the game or its input. While it waits, it uses 0 ms of CPU time over 10 seconds and 6.9 MB of memory. Its effect on the frame rate inside a running game has not been measured yet.

### Can I use an image or a custom shape as a crosshair?

No. Scorpio's crosshairs are CS2-based only: five static styles and their settings.

### Which pro players' crosshairs are included?

Thirteen: ropz, b1t, jL, Twistzz, XANTARES, Spinx, iM, w0nderful, karrigan, apEX, sh1ro, flameZ and mezii. The codes were seen in their matches and confirmed by a second source. The players did not provide or endorse them.

### Does Scorpio collect data or go online?

Scorpio has no telemetry and no account. It makes one kind of request: the update check to this repository's releases on GitHub, and the download of an update. With automatic updates off, it checks only when you click **Check for updates**. The OBS source listens on your own computer only, and only while it is on.

### Where does Scorpio keep its settings?

In one file, `settings.json`. On Windows the file is in `%APPDATA%\com.lxxvii.scorpio`. On Linux it is in `~/.config/com.lxxvii.scorpio`.

### Is Scorpio open source?

The source is public and you can read it, change it and share your own versions for any non-commercial purpose. The licence is PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0, which forbids commercial use, so Scorpio is source-available and not open source in the OSI sense.

### Does Scorpio run on macOS?

No. Scorpio is for Windows and Linux.

## What is tested and what is not

Scorpio is at version 0.1.1. This is the state on 2026-11-21.

| Area | State |
| --- | --- |
| Crosshair maths, share codes, game matching, launcher parsers, app state | Unit tests (`npm test`) |
| Windows build | Built, run and driven on Windows 11. Memory and CPU measured idle and with the settings window |
| Windows, inside a real game session | Not tested yet |
| Linux X11 layer | Run under a virtual X server (Xvfb). The full app has not been run or measured on a Linux desktop |
| Crosshair in OBS | The local server has a test. Not tested inside OBS yet |
| Automatic updates | Written. Not yet exercised against a published release |
| Settings window | Rendered in headless Chromium. Not yet checked in WebKitGTK |
| Any specific anti-cheat | Not tested |

Known gaps:

- A game that turns borderless fullscreen after it came to the front is seen only at the next focus or display change.
- A window that is still invisible when it comes to the front is ignored until the next focus change.
- One added UWP game matches every UWP app, because they share a host process.
- There is no log file yet.

The screenshots on this page show the real settings window in headless Chromium with sample data.

## Build from source

You need Rust 1.80 or newer, Node.js, and the [Tauri 2 prerequisites](https://v2.tauri.app/start/prerequisites/) for your system.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair.git
cd Scorpio-Crosshair
npm install
npm test        # the Rust unit tests
npm run dev     # run from source
npm run build   # build the installer or the packages
```

`npm run build` writes the NSIS installer on Windows, and the AppImage and the `.deb` on Linux, under `src-tauri/target/release/bundle/`.

## How Scorpio is built

One small native process does everything that runs while you play. The settings window is a web view that exists only while it is open.

```
scorpio (Rust, Tauri 2)
├── core/        pure logic with unit tests, no OS calls
│   ├── crosshair    CS2-based settings, share codes, shapes in pixels
│   ├── raster       shapes to a premultiplied RGBA bitmap
│   ├── center       exact centring
│   ├── focus        the two-of-four match
│   └── launchers    install folders from launcher files
├── platform/    the only code that talks to the OS
│   ├── windows      layered overlay window, foreground event, Raw Input
│   └── linux        X11 override-redirect overlay, active-window events, XInput2
├── app          state machine: locked, focus, aiming
├── store        settings file, defaults, validation
├── obs          the Browser source server
├── update       signed updates between games
└── ui/          the settings window (HTML, CSS, JS), opened on demand
```

More detail:

- [docs/plans/scorpio.md](docs/plans/scorpio.md) has the plan, the anti-cheat review of every OS call, and the known gaps.
- [docs/budget.md](docs/budget.md) has the measured memory, CPU and size, with their budgets.
- [docs/style-audit.md](docs/style-audit.md) explains the decisions behind the settings window.
- [assets/brand/README.md](assets/brand/README.md) describes the logo and its files.

## The name and the logo

A Roman *scorpio* was the legion's small torsion bolt-thrower, its precision weapon. The logo is called Nodus. Two long bands, each the shape of a bolt head, are interlaced like the rope of the scorpio's two torsion springs, and they hold one red point: the aim.

## Licence

Scorpio is under the [PolyForm Noncommercial License 1.0.0](LICENSE). You may use it, change it and share your own versions for any non-commercial purpose. Commercial use is not allowed. Copyright 2026 LXXVII.

The fonts Forum and Inter are under the SIL Open Font License 1.1 (`ui/fonts/OFL-Forum.txt`, `ui/fonts/OFL-Inter.txt`). The share code layouts follow the documentation of [akiver/csgo-sharecode](https://github.com/akiver/csgo-sharecode) (MIT).

`assets/brand/README.md`:

```md
# Scorpio: the sign

The sign is abstract. Two long lozenge bands, each the shape of a bolt head, are crossed and interlaced like the rope of the scorpio's two torsion springs, and between them they hold one porphyry point: the aim. It has one band weight, four-way symmetry and straight cuts only, and its four arms and centre work as a crosshair.

The weapon and the legion are the meaning behind the sign, not its picture.

## Files

| File | Use |
| --- | --- |
| `scorpio-mark.svg` | The mark alone, marble with the porphyry point, on a transparent ground. For dark grounds. |
| `scorpio-mark-dark.svg` | The mark in basalt ink with the darker porphyry. For light grounds. |
| `scorpio-lockup.svg`, `scorpio-lockup-dark.svg` | The mark over SCORPIO in Forum, widely spaced. The letters are outlines, so no font is needed. |
| `scorpio-icon.svg` | The app icon: the mark on a stone tablet with cut corners and four bronze cramps. Source of `tools/icon.png`. |
| `scorpio-tray.svg` | The tray icon, redrawn on a 16 px grid. Source of the 16 and 32 px sizes. |
| `scorpio-tray-24.svg` | The same on a 24 px grid. Source of the 24 and 48 px sizes. |
| `scorpio-tray-16.png`, `scorpio-tray-32.png` | The tray icon rendered at 16 and 32 px. |
| `concepts.png`, `concepts/` | The three concepts that were considered, and their vector sources. |

Colours are the style's tokens: marble `#ebe5da`, basalt `#0b0a09`, stone `#141311`, porphyry `#c8435a` on dark and `#9b2c3f` on light, and the bronze of the cramps.

## Small sizes

At 32 px and below the interlace cannot be drawn, so the tray icon is a second drawing of the same silhouette: a four-pointed ring round the point, with every edge on a whole pixel, on the stone tablet so that it reads on light and on dark taskbars.

Tauri gives the tray the 32 px icon (48 px at 150 % scaling) and the shell shrinks it to half. `tools/icon.js --icons` therefore stores the tray drawing at 16, 24, 32 and 48 px in `src-tauri/icons/icon.ico` and in `32x32.png`. The 32 and 48 px sizes are exact doubles of the 16 and 24 px drawings, so they halve without blur. Sizes from 64 px up come from `scorpio-icon.svg`.

## Rebuild

```
node tools/icon.js            # tools/icon.png and the two tray PNGs
node tools/icon.js --icons    # the same, then src-tauri/icons
```

The script needs a headless Chromium. It looks in the Playwright cache and the usual install places; set `CHROME` to use another binary. `--icons` runs `npx tauri icon tools/icon.png` and deletes the Android, iOS, Windows Store and macOS outputs, because Scorpio ships for Windows and Linux only.

## The concepts

![Three concepts at several sizes](concepts.png)

1. **Nodus** (chosen). The interlaced bands. It is the only one of the three that is a sign of its own and not a pattern, and it keeps its silhouette down to 16 px.
2. **Acies.** Four bolt heads aimed at one point. The simplest, but close to a compass rose.
3. **Quincunx.** Five tesserae set as the legion set its maniples. It reads at any size, but as a tile.

## Licence of the letters

Forum is under the SIL Open Font License 1.1 (`ui/fonts/OFL-Forum.txt`). The licence allows the font to be used in artwork, and outlines in a logo are not a copy of the font.

`docs/budget.md`:

```md
# Budget

What Scorpio costs while you play, measured on the owner's Windows 11
gaming PC (2026-11-21, release build, 2560x1440). `tools/measure-windows.ps1`
fails when a figure grows by more than 25 %. Lower a budget on purpose, in
its own commit, after the figure improved.

| Figure | Measured | Budget |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Private memory, idle in the tray, settings never opened | 6.9 MB | 8.6 MB |
| Private memory, after the settings window was opened and closed | 10.2 MB | 12.8 MB |
| CPU time over 10 s idle | 0 ms | 50 ms |
| Web view processes with the settings window closed | 0 | 0 |
| `scorpio.exe` size | 3.7 MB | 4.6 MB |

With the settings window open, WebView2 adds six processes and about
240 MB; all of them exit when the window closes.

Linux (VPS, Ubuntu 24.04 under Xvfb + openbox, the .deb build, settings
never opened, 2026-11-21): GTK and WebKitGTK are loaded at start, so Linux
costs more than Windows.

| Figure | Measured | Budget |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Private dirty memory (anonymous) | 20.1 MB | 25 MB |
| PSS (shared libraries counted fairly) | 70.7 MB | 88 MB |
| CPU over 10 s idle | 2 ticks (20 ms) | 5 ticks |
| .deb size | 3.2 MB | 4 MB |
| AppImage size (bundles WebKitGTK) | 78 MB | 98 MB |

Measured with `/proc/<pid>/smaps_rollup` and `/proc/<pid>/stat`; there is
no Linux check script yet.

```

`docs/plans/scorpio.md`:

```md
# Scorpio: plan

A crosshair overlay for Windows and Linux, written new, with what CrossOver
became in its `game-focus` branch as a reference for behaviour only. Started
2026-11-21 as an overnight run while the owner sleeps.

## What the owner asked for, in priority order

1. **Anti-cheat safety first.** Scorpio never touches a game's or an
   anti-cheat's process: no handles to it, no injection, no reading its memory,
   no hooks in it, no synthetic input. Anything that could make an anti-cheat
   look at Scorpio is out.
2. **Light, fast, stable.** Minimal memory and CPU, nothing polling that
   doesn't need to.
3. **UX that is logical and obvious.**
4. Linux and Windows only. CS2's crosshair system only (no image presets).
   Remove features that don't earn their place (theme choice and so on).
5. No checkboxes: switches, dropdowns and the like. No square shapes next to
   rounded ones: one shape language.
6. Written from scratch. Licence: open source, no commercial use; anyone may
   use, change and publish their own versions.

## Architecture

One small native process does everything that runs while you play; the
settings interface is a web view that exists only while it is open.

```
scorpio (Rust, Tauri 2)
├── core/            pure logic, unit-tested, no OS calls
│   ├── crosshair    CS2 settings, share codes, shapes in pixels
│   ├── raster       shapes -> premultiplied RGBA bitmap
│   ├── center       exact centring
│   ├── focus        2-of-4 matching, focus state machine
│   └── launchers    install folders from launcher files
├── platform/        the only code that talks to the OS
│   ├── windows      layered overlay window, foreground event hook,
│   │                Raw Input, process image path without a handle
│   └── linux        X11 override-redirect overlay, active-window events,
│                    XInput2 raw events
├── app              state machine: locked, focus, ADS -> overlay
├── store            settings file, defaults, validation
└── ui/              settings web view (HTML, CSS, JS), opened on demand
```

### Anti-cheat review of every OS call

| Need | Windows | Linux (X11) | Why it is safe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Window in front | `SetWinEventHook(EVENT_SYSTEM_FOREGROUND, WINEVENT_OUTOFCONTEXT)` | `PropertyNotify` on the root's `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` | Out of context: nothing is loaded into any other process. Event driven: no polling. |
| Its pid, title, class, fullscreen | `GetWindowThreadProcessId`, `GetWindowTextW`, `GetClassNameW`, `GetWindowRect`, `MonitorFromWindow` | `_NET_WM_PID`, `WM_CLASS`, `_NET_WM_NAME`, `_NET_WM_STATE` | Window manager data; no process is opened. |
| Its executable | `NtQuerySystemInformation(SystemProcessIdInformation)` | `/proc/<pid>/exe`, else `cmdline` for Wine | No process handle is ever opened (`OpenProcess` is banned). |
| Aim button, hide key | Raw Input, `RIDEV_INPUTSINK`, on Scorpio's own hidden window | XInput2 raw events on the root | No low-level hooks (`WH_*_LL` are banned): the same input every game and Discord read. |
| Hotkeys | `RegisterHotKey` | `XGrabKey` | Standard. |
| Overlay | Layered, topmost, click-through window (`WS_EX_LAYERED|TRANSPARENT|TOOLWINDOW|NOACTIVATE`) | Override-redirect ARGB window, empty input shape | A separate window over the game, like Discord's or Steam's. Never drawn into the game. |
| Colour pipette | One screen grab when the player asks | Same | Only on request. |
| Crosshair in OBS (opt-in) | A local HTTP server on 127.0.0.1:47117 serving a Browser source page and server-sent events | Same | Talks only to OBS on the same computer; no game involved. Off by default; no thread or socket while off. |
| Hide from screen capture (owner's exception, 2026-11-21) | `SetWindowDisplayAffinity(WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE)` on the overlay | Not available | **Not risk-free**: cheat overlays use the same call to hide from anti-cheat screenshots. Off by default; the switch says so. Kept at the owner's request. |

Banned outright: `OpenProcess` on other processes, `ReadProcessMemory`,
`WriteProcessMemory`, DLL injection, `SetWindowsHookEx` of any kind,
`SendInput`/`keybd_event`, kernel drivers, DirectX/Vulkan hooks, scanning
other processes' modules.

### Lightness budget

While playing: one process, target under 20 MB private memory, 0% CPU when
nothing changes (every input is an event, not a poll). The settings web view
is created when opened and destroyed when closed. Measured and recorded in
`docs/budget.md`; a check fails when it grows by more than 25%.

## Steps

1. [x] Licence, plan, JS reference of the core (to be ported).
2. [x] Rust workspace and interfaces (core types, platform traits).
3. [x] Core in Rust with tests (crosshair, raster, center, focus, launchers).
4. [x] Windows platform layer (built and probed on the gaming PC).
5. [x] Linux platform layer (built and probed on the VPS under Xvfb).
6. [x] App state machine, store, tray, hotkeys, Tauri commands.
7. [x] Settings UI: tabs Crosshair, Games, Controls, System (see the style audit);
       switches, dropdowns, sliders, colour picker, keybind fields; one
       shape language.
8. [x] Style and UX audit (`docs/style-audit.md`), applied.
9. [x] Windows: build on the gaming PC, run, drive, measure (idle and settings; not yet in a real game).
10. [x] Linux: .deb and AppImage built on the VPS, run under Xvfb + openbox, measured (settings window not driven yet).
11. [x] Independent review (Opus, Grok paused), fixes; open items under Known gaps.

## Dropped from CrossOver's feature set

Image presets, custom images, tint, reticle overlay, SVG customisation, theme
choice, slab colours, app size, duplicate crosshairs, follow mouse, tilt,
sounds, update checks, announcements, macOS, portable mode.

## Known gaps (from the 2026-11-21 review)

- A game that turns borderless fullscreen after it came to the front is
  seen only at the next focus or display change (Windows watches only
  foreground and display changes; location events would wake Scorpio on
  every cursor move).
- A window that is still invisible when it comes to the front is ignored
  until the next focus change.
- One added UWP game (ApplicationFrameHost) matches every UWP app.
- No log file: failures such as a settings write error are silent.
- With hide-while-aiming on, raw mouse input wakes the platform thread for
  every movement packet. Not yet measured; add a figure to `docs/budget.md`.

## Code signing (owner's decision, 2026-11-21)

No paid code signing certificate: the cost is not worth it for this
project. The Windows installer stays unsigned, so SmartScreen warns on
first run (the README and release notes say how to continue). Once the
owner has tested Scorpio and the fixes from that test are released, the
installer of that version is submitted to Microsoft for analysis, once,
not for every small release.

```

<section class="block" id="games" aria-labelledby="games-h">
	<div class="copy rv">
		<h2 class="name" id="games-h">Only over games</h2>
		
		<p>The crosshair shows while a game is the window in front and hides when you switch away. A game is a window <b>you added</b>, any <b>fullscreen</b> window, or a game from a launcher such as Steam, Epic Games or GOG.</p>
		<p>It can also hide while you aim, and global shortcuts lock, centre and switch it.</p>
	</div>
	<figure class="shot"><img src="images/scorpio-game-detection.webp" width="1960" height="1400" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="Scorpio Games tab: two added games, one with its own crosshair, and switches for fullscreen windows and games from launchers"></figure>
</section>

<section class="block flip" id="crosshair" aria-labelledby="crosshair-h">
	<div class="copy rv">
		<h2 class="name" id="crosshair-h">The crosshair</h2>
		
		<p><b>CS2 crosshair codes work in Scorpio</b>: paste a share code, or copy yours. <b>CS2-based crosshairs</b> come in five static styles, with thickness in half steps and an outline of 1 to 6 pixels.</p>
		<p>Keep your own crosshairs, pick one of 13 pro players, or give each game its own.</p>
	</div>
	<figure class="shot"><img src="images/scorpio-saved-and-pro-crosshairs.webp" width="1960" height="1400" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="Scorpio Crosshair tab: a green crosshair in the preview at 4x, its share code, four saved crosshairs and pro player crosshairs"></figure>
</section>

<section class="block" id="obs" aria-labelledby="obs-h">
	<div class="copy rv">
		<h2 class="name" id="obs-h">On stream</h2>
		
		<p>Game Capture records only the game, so add Scorpio as a <b>Browser source</b>.</p>
		<ol class="steps">
			<li>In Scorpio, on the System tab, switch on <b>Show the crosshair in OBS</b>.</li>
			<li>In OBS, add a Browser source above your game capture.</li>
			<li>Set its URL to <code>http://127.0.0.1:47117/obs</code>.</li>
			<li>Make it the size of your canvas, with no transform.</li>
		</ol>
	</div>
	<figure class="shot"><img src="images/scorpio-obs-browser-source.webp" width="1960" height="1400" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="Scorpio System tab: Show the crosshair in OBS is on, with three setup steps and the Browser source address"></figure>
</section>

<section class="block wide" id="safety" aria-labelledby="safety-h">
	<h2 class="name" id="safety-h">Outside the game</h2>
	<p class="lead">Scorpio never opens another process, reads or writes its memory, injects code, installs a hook, sends synthetic input, loads a driver, or draws into the game's frames.</p>
	<table class="tbl obj">
		<thead><tr><th scope="col">Need</th><th scope="col">Windows</th><th scope="col">Linux (X11)</th><th scope="col">Why it stays outside the game</th></tr></thead>
		<tbody>
			<tr><td>Window in front</td><td data-h="Windows"><code>SetWinEventHook</code>, out of context</td><td data-h="Linux (X11)"><code>_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW</code> events</td><td>Nothing is loaded into another process</td></tr>
			<tr><td>Title, class and size</td><td data-h="Windows"><code>GetWindowTextW</code>, <code>GetClassNameW</code>, <code>GetWindowRect</code></td><td data-h="Linux (X11)">Window manager properties</td><td>No process is opened</td></tr>
			<tr><td>Program path</td><td data-h="Windows"><code>NtQuerySystemInformation</code></td><td data-h="Linux (X11)"><code>/proc/&lt;pid&gt;/exe</code></td><td>No process handle</td></tr>
			<tr><td>Aim button, hide key</td><td data-h="Windows">Raw Input on its own window</td><td data-h="Linux (X11)">XInput2 raw events</td><td>No hooks</td></tr>
			<tr><td>Shortcuts</td><td data-h="Windows"><code>RegisterHotKey</code></td><td data-h="Linux (X11)"><code>XGrabKey</code></td><td>The standard shortcut API</td></tr>
			<tr><td>The crosshair</td><td data-h="Windows">A layered, topmost, click-through window</td><td data-h="Linux (X11)">An override-redirect ARGB window</td><td>Nothing is drawn into the game</td></tr>
			<tr><td>OBS, opt-in</td><td data-h="Windows">HTTP on <code>127.0.0.1:47117</code></td><td data-h="Linux (X11)">The same</td><td>Your own computer only</td></tr>
		</tbody>
	</table>
	<div class="faq obj" id="faq">
		<details class="qa"><summary>Is a crosshair overlay bannable?<i aria-hidden="true"></i></summary><div class="a"><p>No overlay can promise that you will never be banned. Scorpio never reads or changes the game, but a game's own rules can forbid external crosshairs.</p></div></details>
		<details class="qa"><summary>Does it work with Vanguard, Easy Anti-Cheat, BattlEye or VAC?<i aria-hidden="true"></i></summary><div class="a"><p>None of them has approved it directly. Scorpio crosses none of their lines, since it never touches the game or the anti-cheat. The exception is a game whose own rules forbid crosshair overlays.</p></div></details>
		<details class="qa"><summary>Does Scorpio lower my frame rate?<i aria-hidden="true"></i></summary><div class="a"><p>It does not touch the game or its input. Its effect on the frame rate has not been measured yet.</p></div></details>
		<details class="qa"><summary>Does Scorpio collect data?<i aria-hidden="true"></i></summary><div class="a"><p>No. It has no telemetry and no account, and goes online only for updates from GitHub.</p></div></details>
	</div>
</section>

		<ol class="steps">
			<li>On Windows 10 or 11, run the <code>-setup.exe</code>. It needs no administrator rights; it is unsigned, so SmartScreen may warn you the first time.</li>

			<li>Click the tray icon, paste a share code, and switch to your game.</li>
		</ol>

<script>
// FAQ answers open and close with their height, on the guide's one curve.
// Reduced motion leaves the native, instant toggle alone.
for ( const d of document.querySelectorAll( '.qa' ) ) {
	const s = d.querySelector( 'summary' ), a = d.querySelector( '.a' )
	let run = null
	s.addEventListener( 'click', e => {
		if ( matchMedia( '(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)' ).matches ) return
		e.preventDefault()
		const from = run ? a.offsetHeight : ( d.open ? a.scrollHeight : 0 )
		run?.cancel()
		const opening = !d.open || ( run && run.closing )
		d.open = true
		const to = opening ? a.scrollHeight : 0
		run = a.animate( [ { height: from + 'px', opacity: opening ? 0 : 1 }, { height: to + 'px', opacity: opening ? 1 : 0 } ],
			{ duration: opening ? 400 : 300, easing: 'cubic-bezier(.2,.7,.15,1)' } )
		run.closing = !opening
		run.onfinish = () => { if ( !opening ) d.open = false; run = null }
	} )
}
(() => {
	if (!('IntersectionObserver' in window)) { document.querySelectorAll('.rv').forEach((el) => el.classList.add('in')); return }
	const rv = new IntersectionObserver((es) => es.forEach((e) => { if (e.isIntersecting) { e.target.classList.add('in'); rv.unobserve(e.target) } }), { threshold: .15 })
	document.querySelectorAll('.rv').forEach((el) => rv.observe(el))
	const lit = new IntersectionObserver((es) => es.forEach((e) => e.target.classList.toggle('current', e.isIntersecting)), { rootMargin: '-40% 0px -40% 0px' })
	document.querySelectorAll('.block').forEach((el) => lit.observe(el))
})()
</script>

`docs/site/llms.txt`:

```txt
# Scorpio

> Scorpio is a free crosshair overlay for PC games on Windows and Linux. It draws a CS2-based crosshair in its own transparent window over the game, only while a game is the window in front. It never opens, reads, hooks or injects into the game or its anti-cheat. It is written in Rust with Tauri 2. The licence is PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0: source-available, free for non-commercial use, not open source in the OSI sense.

Facts to get right:

- Version 0.1.1, state of 2026-11-21. Made by LXXVII. Also written "Scorpio Crosshair".
- Systems: Windows 10 and 11, Linux with X11 or XWayland. No native Wayland windows, no macOS.
- CS2-based crosshairs in five static styles (Static Cross, Static Circle, Dot Only, Static Square, Static Quad). No image or dynamic crosshairs. A dynamic code shows as its static resting style.
- CS2 crosshair codes work in Scorpio, in and out (codes start with `CSGO-` or `CS`).
- Thickness in half steps and an outline of 1 to 6 pixels. Share codes carry neither.
- 13 pro player crosshairs, seen in matches. The players did not provide or endorse them.
- A game is a window the player added (two of four checks match: program name, program path, window title, window class), a fullscreen window, or a game from a launcher: Steam, Epic Games, GOG, Ubisoft Connect, EA, Battle.net, Riot and Xbox on Windows; Steam, Heroic and Lutris on Linux.
- Also: a crosshair per game, saved crosshairs, exact centring on the game's monitor, hide while aiming (raw input), global shortcuts, an opt-in OBS Browser source at `http://127.0.0.1:47117/obs`, and signed automatic updates that install only after no game has been in front for 3 minutes.
- Weight on Windows 11: 6.9 MB of private memory and 0 ms of CPU over 10 s while idle. `scorpio.exe` is 3.7 MB.
- Anti-cheat: Scorpio never opens another process, reads or writes its memory, injects code, installs hooks, sends synthetic input, loads a driver, or draws into the game's frames. No anti-cheat vendor has approved it directly, but it crosses none of their lines: it never touches the game or the anti-cheat. The exception is a game whose own rules forbid crosshair overlays. Do not call it approved, whitelisted or guaranteed safe.
- "Hide from screen capture" (Windows only, off by default) is not risk-free: cheat overlays use the same Windows call.
- Not tested yet: inside a real game, inside OBS, the full app on a Linux desktop, and automatic updates against a published release.

## Docs

- [README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/main/README.md): Features, every OS call, install, OBS, FAQ, test status, building.
- [Plan and anti-cheat review](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/main/docs/plans/scorpio.md): Architecture, the OS call review, banned calls, known gaps.
- [Budget](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/main/docs/budget.md): Measured memory, CPU and file size, with enforced budgets.

## Download and source

- [Latest release](https://github.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/releases/latest): Windows installer, Linux AppImage and `.deb`.
- [Source code](https://github.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair): The GitHub repository.
- [Licence](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/main/LICENSE): PolyForm Noncommercial License 1.0.0.
- [Website](https://scorpio.lxxvll.com/): The landing page, with the FAQ as structured data.

## Optional

- [Style and UX audit](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/main/docs/style-audit.md): Decisions behind the settings window.
- [Logo and brand files](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Deikus-LXXVII/Scorpio-Crosshair/main/assets/brand/README.md): The Nodus sign and its files.

`docs/style-audit.md`:

```md
# Style and UX audit

Scorpio's settings window, checked against the owner's rules and the
lxxvii standard style (`~/.claude/skills/lxxvii-style`, published in the
private LXXVII-Design repository). Written 2026-11-21.

## Rules from the owner

| Rule | How Scorpio meets it | Proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No checkboxes | Every on/off is a `.switch` (`role="switch"`). Choices of two or three are segmented controls, longer ones drawn dropdowns. The page has no `<input type="checkbox">` or `type="radio"` | `grep -n 'checkbox\|radio"' ui/*.js ui/*.html` finds only `role="radio"` on segmented buttons |
| No square next to rounded | One shape language: the kit's 2px cut-stone corner on every control and container, 1px on parts under 20px, no pills or discs. The only round things are status points of 10px or less | `ui/components.css` header; `border-radius` values in `ui/app.css` are none |
| Remove what doesn't earn its place | Theme choice, image presets, custom images, tint, reticle, app size, sounds, follow mouse, tilt, duplicates, update checks: gone (see the plan) | `docs/plans/scorpio.md` |
| Logical, obvious UX | See below | Screenshots of every tab, rendered headless with a mocked IPC |

## Decisions

- **Four tabs, not six.** The plan listed Crosshair, Games, Actions,
  Keybinds, System, About. Actions held one setting (hide while aiming) and
  About one paragraph, so Actions and Keybinds became **Controls**, and
  About became the foot of **System**. Fewer places to look.
- **The crosshair is edited where it is seen.** The preview sits on the
  left at actual size (SVG units are physical pixels, divided by the
  device pixel ratio), with a 4× view for detail. Controls scroll beside
  it; the preview stays.
- **One editor for every crosshair.** A game with its own crosshair is
  edited in the same Crosshair tab, chosen in a dropdown above the
  preview that appears only when such a game exists. No second editor, no
  extra layer of windows (the owner's complaint about CrossOver).
- **Share codes are one field.** Paste to import, Copy to export. The
  outline width is kept on import, because CS2 codes don't carry it, and
  the help line says so.
- **Saved crosshairs are tiles.** Click applies; the picked tile shows
  rename, Update and Delete beneath the grid. The next and previous
  shortcuts walk the same list.
- **Status lives in the title bar.** One line says what the overlay is
  doing now: shown over which game, hidden until a game is in front,
  hidden by shortcut, or unlocked for moving.
- **Shortcuts can't steal game keys.** A shortcut needs Ctrl, Alt or
  Super unless it is an F-key. A shortcut another program holds is
  marked and explained.
- **Platform honesty.** "Hide from screen capture" is disabled on Linux
  with the reason, instead of a switch that does nothing.
- **Motion.** Only the kit's state changes (colour .3s, thumbs .4s, one
  curve). The overlay itself never animates: it appears and disappears
  at once, because a fading crosshair is a wrong crosshair. The kit's
  `prefers-reduced-motion` block switches transitions off.

## Gaps

- Not yet seen in the real web views (WebView2 on Windows, WebKitGTK on
  Linux). The headless render is Chromium, like WebView2; WebKitGTK may
  differ in slider styling.
- The colour pipette uses the web view's `EyeDropper`. WebView2 has it;
  WebKitGTK does not, so on Linux the Pick button is absent.

```

`src-tauri/Cargo.toml`:

```toml
[package]
name = "scorpio"
version = "0.1.1"
description = "A CS2 crosshair overlay for Windows and Linux that shows only while your game is in focus"
license-file = "../LICENSE"
edition = "2021"
rust-version = "1.80"

[lib]
name = "scorpio_lib"
crate-type = ["staticlib", "cdylib", "rlib"]

[build-dependencies]
tauri-build = { version = "2", features = [] }

[dependencies]
tauri = { version = "2", features = ["tray-icon"] }
tauri-plugin-global-shortcut = "2"
tauri-plugin-autostart = "2"
tauri-plugin-single-instance = "2"
tauri-plugin-updater = "2"
serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }
serde_json = "1"
log = "0.4"

[target.'cfg(windows)'.dependencies]
windows = { version = "0.62", features = [
  "Win32_Foundation",
  "Win32_Graphics_Dwm",
  "Win32_Graphics_Gdi",
  "Win32_System_LibraryLoader",
  "Win32_System_Threading",
  "Win32_System_WindowsProgramming",
  "Win32_UI_Accessibility",
  "Win32_UI_HiDpi",
  "Win32_UI_Input",
  "Win32_UI_Input_KeyboardAndMouse",
  "Win32_UI_WindowsAndMessaging",
  "Wdk_System_SystemInformation",
  "Win32_Storage_FileSystem",
] }

[target.'cfg(target_os = "linux")'.dependencies]
x11rb = { version = "0.14", features = ["xinput", "shape", "randr"] }

[profile.release]
codegen-units = 1
lto = true
opt-level = "s"
panic = "abort"
strip = true

```

fn main() {
    tauri_build::build()
}

`src-tauri/capabilities/default.json`:

```json
{
  "$schema": "../gen/schemas/desktop-schema.json",
  "identifier": "default",
  "description": "The settings window may call Scorpio's own commands and nothing else",
  "windows": [
    "settings"
  ],
  "permissions": [
    "core:default",
    "core:window:allow-minimize",
    "core:window:allow-toggle-maximize",
    "core:window:allow-close",
    "core:window:allow-start-dragging",
    "core:window:allow-is-maximized"
  ]
}

```

`src-tauri/examples/platform_probe.rs`:

```rs
//! Drives the platform layer from stdin so a test harness can check it from
//! outside. Prints every event as `EVENT <debug>`. Commands, one per line:
//!   show <w> <h> <x> <y> locked|unlocked   (button = the middle third; unlocked draws a slab)
//!   hide | list | quit
//!   listen <buttons,comma> <keys,comma>     (e.g. `listen Right,Back H,Shift`)
//!   inspect <window id>                     (Linux: shapes and override-redirect)

use scorpio_lib::core::raster::Bitmap;
use scorpio_lib::platform::{self, Command, MouseButton};
use std::io::{BufRead, Write};

/// A red cross; `slab` fills the rest with translucent dark grey, like the
/// unlocked slab does.
fn cross(width: u32, height: u32, slab: bool) -> Bitmap {
    let mut bitmap = Bitmap::transparent(width, height);
    if slab {
        bitmap.data.chunks_exact_mut(4).for_each(|p| p.copy_from_slice(&[7, 6, 7, 158]));
    }
    for y in 0..height {
        for x in 0..width {
            if x == width / 2 || y == height / 2 {
                let i = ((y * width + x) * 4) as usize;
                bitmap.data[i..i + 4].copy_from_slice(&[0, 0, 255, 255]);
            }
        }
    }
    bitmap
}

fn button(name: &str) -> Option<MouseButton> {
    Some(match name {
        "Left" => MouseButton::Left,
        "Right" => MouseButton::Right,
        "Middle" => MouseButton::Middle,
        "Back" => MouseButton::Back,
        "Forward" => MouseButton::Forward,
        _ => return None,
    })
}

fn list(arg: Option<&str>) -> Vec<&str> {
    arg.unwrap_or("").split(',').filter(|s| !s.is_empty() && *s != "-").collect()
}

fn main() {
    let (sender, events) = std::sync::mpsc::channel();
    let platform = match platform::start(sender) {
        Ok(p) => p,
        Err(e) => {
            println!("UNSUPPORTED {}", e.0);
            return;
        }
    };
    std::thread::spawn(move || {
        for event in events {
            println!("EVENT {event:?}");
            std::io::stdout().flush().ok();
        }
    });
    for line in std::io::stdin().lock().lines() {
        let line = line.unwrap_or_default();
        let words: Vec<&str> = line.split_whitespace().collect();
        let num = |i: usize| words.get(i).and_then(|w| w.parse::<i32>().ok()).unwrap_or(0);
        match words.first().copied() {
            Some("show") => {
                let (w, h) = (num(1), num(2));
                let unlocked = words.get(5) == Some(&"unlocked");
                platform.send(Command::Show {
                    bitmap: cross(w as u32, h as u32, unlocked),
                    x: num(3),
                    y: num(4),
                    unlocked,
                    button: Some((w / 3, h / 3, w / 3, h / 3)),
                });
            }
            Some("hide") => platform.send(Command::Hide),
            Some("listen") => platform.send(Command::ListenTo {
                mouse: list(words.get(1).copied()).into_iter().filter_map(button).collect(),
                keys: list(words.get(2).copied()).into_iter().map(String::from).collect(),
            }),
            Some("list") => {
                for window in (platform.list_windows)() {
                    println!("WINDOW {window:?}");
                }
            }
            Some("inspect") => inspect(words.get(1).copied().unwrap_or("0")),
            Some("quit") => {
                platform.send(Command::Quit);
                break;
            }
            _ => println!("? {line}"),
        }
        println!("OK {line}");
        std::io::stdout().flush().ok();
    }
}

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
fn inspect(id: &str) {
    use x11rb::connection::Connection;
    use x11rb::protocol::shape::{ConnectionExt as _, SK};
    use x11rb::protocol::xproto::ConnectionExt as _;
    let id = u32::from_str_radix(id.trim_start_matches("0x"), 16).unwrap_or(0);
    let (conn, _) = x11rb::connect(None).expect("connect");
    let rects = |kind| conn.shape_get_rectangles(id, kind).ok().and_then(|c| c.reply().ok()).map(|r| r.rectangles.len());
    let attrs = conn.get_window_attributes(id).ok().and_then(|c| c.reply().ok());
    println!(
        "INSPECT input_rects={:?} bounding_rects={:?} override_redirect={:?} depth={:?}",
        rects(SK::INPUT),
        rects(SK::BOUNDING),
        attrs.map(|a| a.override_redirect),
        conn.get_geometry(id).ok().and_then(|c| c.reply().ok()).map(|g| g.depth),
    );
    let _ = conn.flush();
}

#[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
fn inspect(_: &str) {}

```

`src-tauri/examples/windows_probe.rs`:

```rs
//! Drives the platform layer for a few seconds and writes what it saw to the
//! file named by the first argument (or stdout): the window in front, the
//! window list, and whether the overlay window was created and destroyed.
//! Windows only; `cargo run --example windows_probe -- probe.txt`.

#[cfg(not(windows))]
fn main() {
    eprintln!("windows_probe only does something on Windows");
}

#[cfg(windows)]
fn main() {
    probe::run();
}

#[cfg(windows)]
mod probe {
    use scorpio_lib::core::raster::Bitmap;
    use scorpio_lib::platform::{start, Command, MouseButton};
    use std::fmt::Write as _;
    use std::sync::mpsc::channel;
    use std::sync::Mutex;
    use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
    use windows::core::w;
    use windows::Win32::UI::WindowsAndMessaging::{FindWindowW, IsWindowVisible};

    static REPORT: Mutex<String> = Mutex::new(String::new());

    fn note(line: String) {
        let _ = writeln!(REPORT.lock().unwrap(), "{line}");
    }

    /// Collects the platform layer's warnings into the report.
    struct Warnings;
    impl log::Log for Warnings {
        fn enabled(&self, _: &log::Metadata) -> bool {
            true
        }
        fn log(&self, record: &log::Record) {
            note(format!("LOG {} {}", record.level(), record.args()));
        }
        fn flush(&self) {}
    }

    fn overlay_state() -> &'static str {
        // SAFETY: looks a window up by class name and reads its visibility; read-only.
        unsafe {
            match FindWindowW(w!("ScorpioOverlay"), None) {
                Ok(hwnd) if IsWindowVisible(hwnd).as_bool() => "exists, visible",
                Ok(_) => "exists, hidden",
                Err(_) => "does not exist",
            }
        }
    }

    pub fn run() {
        let _ = log::set_logger(&Warnings);
        log::set_max_level(log::LevelFilter::Trace);
        let begun = Instant::now();
        let (events, received) = channel();
        let platform = match start(events) {
            Ok(platform) => platform,
            Err(error) => {
                note(format!("START FAILED {error:?}"));
                return finish();
            }
        };
        note(format!("started in {:?}; own pid {}; overlay {}", begun.elapsed(), std::process::id(), overlay_state()));

        // A faint 32x32 square, premultiplied: alpha 64, colour scaled to match.
        let mut bitmap = Bitmap::transparent(32, 32);
        for pixel in bitmap.data.chunks_exact_mut(4) {
            pixel.copy_from_slice(&[0, 64, 0, 64]);
        }
        let pause = |ms| std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(ms));
        platform.send(Command::Raise);
        platform.send(Command::Show { bitmap: bitmap.clone(), x: 200, y: 200, unlocked: false, button: None });
        pause(400);
        note(format!("after locked show: overlay {}", overlay_state()));
        platform.send(Command::ListenTo { mouse: vec![MouseButton::Right], keys: vec!["H".into(), "Mouse4".into()] });
        platform.send(Command::Show { bitmap, x: 240, y: 240, unlocked: true, button: Some((12, 12, 8, 8)) });
        pause(400);
        note(format!("after unlocked show: overlay {}", overlay_state()));
        platform.send(Command::ListenTo { mouse: vec![], keys: vec![] });
        platform.send(Command::Raise);
        platform.send(Command::Hide);
        pause(200);
        note(format!("after hide: overlay {}", overlay_state()));

        let listed = Instant::now();
        let windows = (platform.list_windows)();
        note(format!("list_windows: {} windows in {:?}", windows.len(), listed.elapsed()));
        for window in &windows {
            note(format!("  {window:?}"));
        }

        platform.send(Command::Quit);
        pause(300);
        note(format!("after quit: overlay {}", overlay_state()));
        for event in received.try_iter() {
            note(format!("EVENT {event:?}"));
        }
        finish();
    }

    fn finish() {
        let report = REPORT.lock().unwrap();
        match std::env::args().nth(1) {
            Some(path) => std::fs::write(path, report.as_bytes()).expect("the report could not be written"),
            None => print!("{report}"),
        }
    }
}
